## Current State Dashboard

Keep this section current in place at every checkpoint, then add a short dated entry below it.

### Target experience (decided 2026-10-09)

The person installs the extension and starts the MCP server in VS Code, opens a Gemini chat in the browser that has the extension (container Chromium for now, D12) and clicks **Connect this chat** once. From then on the agent reads and sends in that chat without further intervention. Closing the tab, switching to another chat, or restarting the browser or VS Code ends the connection; reloading the same chat keeps it. D11a permits an already approved autonomous chat to operate hidden without activating its tab; supervised operations remain visible-only. Hidden behavior is source/synthetic gated with one bounded person-confirmed installed background send, not general reliability acceptance. Other sites follow through the neutral pipeline (D5). No provider APIs, credential export or unrestricted browser control.

Clarification (2026-10-28, D15): supported integrations must use unmodified websites. Site patches, custom frontend builds and redeployment are not setup prerequisites. D16 proposes a stock Databag binding investigation; no exception to D1/D2 or other safety boundaries has been approved.

Compatibility baseline (2026-10-29, D17): no legacy Databag support is required. The person now reports a normal upstream update to 0.1.18 and supplies metadata consistent with the newer frontend; resume offline current-UI binding work, not legacy support. Exact source/image and loaded extension Build remain unverified. This was person-managed maintenance, not an integration patch or agent deployment. D16 stays open; an upgrade is not operational acceptance or new authority.

Revision (2026-10-12, D13a, D13b): multiple agents may connect to the same chat, each approved once in the trusted popup for autonomous access. Show the count and distinguishable identities and allow individual removal without affecting the others. Separate grants and request/operation ownership remain mandatory; shared-composer writes are serialized. No automatic admission of later arrivals or silent re-admission after removal.

### Capability matrix

Databag entries describe marker-free synthetic coverage accepted by the exact 248-unit/141-named-E2E gate. They are not deployed/installed capability. Default origin is unset; do not deploy a patch. Reported 0.1.18/newer layout does not attest exact source/ABI or loaded Build. D17 excludes legacy work; D20 permits bounded implementation/synthetics. All older live scopes ended.

| Public tool | Local fixture | Gemini saved chat | Databag native (synthetic only) |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |

Bounded same-chat reload passed with the original handle and fresh cursor epoch, followed by disconnect/stale | Popup-first only; owned get/disconnect covered; no legacy pending approval |
| `chat_wait_for_connection` and popup **Connect this chat** | Works; installed single-owner pickup/removal verified | Works; installed single-owner approval/pickup/removal verified | Exact configured-origin admission, pickup and per-owner removal |

| `chat_wait_for_reply` | Two-second heuristic and bounded waits SDK/browser gated; bounded installed run passed | New four-case math wait on person-confirmed image-backed Build returns a new complete/untruncated reply, distinct from baseline and exactly matching two later captures. Indexed/nested structure preserved for this sample. Earlier evidence/failures remain; UI completion is not provider delivery or complete fidelity | Public two-second `settled_heuristic`; no Databag completion signal |
| `chat_send_message` | Sandboxed flows cover own-window/hidden sends, hidden human-draft refusal, revocation/cancellation, hidden closure/navigation/freeze expiry and bounded visible own-leftover replacement; bounded installed run passed | Latest exact separately approved math prompt returned PAGE_SENT, retryAllowed false, followed by a new complete reply and two matching captures within original limits, then disconnect/stale. No replay/replacement or delivery/general-reliability claim. Older uncertain attempt untouched | Public send/replay and human-draft refusal; dropped-result cases execute once |
| `chat_prepare_message` | Works | Public tool refuses with `CONNECTION_NOT_FOUND`; private `prepare_gemini_message` works | Public supervised tool unsupported; neutral autonomous preparation only |
| `chat_fill_draft` | Works; installed textarea verified | Not public; private fill and worker connected, synthetic tests only | Public supervised tool unsupported; autonomous worker connected |
| `chat_commit_message` | Works; installed textarea verified (`observed_in_ui`) | Not public; private neutral supervised commit/submit connected, synthetic only | Public supervised tool unsupported; use autonomous send |
| `chat_get_operation` | Works; receipt recovery is status only | Works for public autonomous and private operations; receipt recovery is status only | Owned UI-evidence status; no token reissue or write authority |

Databag (D14-D20): native source uses reviewed bounded React identity/readiness and rendered row metadata without website markers. Its exact full gate now passes; the earlier Gemini timeout and overlapping runs remain failed history with unproven individual causes. Deployed/installed verification and D16 remain open. Prior patched-contract evidence stays historical. No installed, genuine hidden or repeated-reload write acceptance; default origin remains unset.

The trusted popup routes generic pending-request approval, ordinary review, fill consent and send consent through the selected adapter; legacy fixture/Gemini commands remain internal and Databag supervised controls stay hidden. Popup-first grants authorize public reads/events and `chat_send_message` without per-message consents. The current exact gate covers this generic UI and native Databag synthetic cases. Supervised controls still require the separate legacy finite grant; inspection never creates or renews it. Databag has no installed acceptance.

The popup shows facade labels/IDs, exact-chat connected counts, per-agent **Connect this chat**, **Remove access** and **Disconnect all**. It requests optional origin permission from the trusted click; before script access, it shows available owners with chat status unknown, not a proven zero count. New grants are `ready`, read/send-capable and `expiresAt: null`; legacy grants remain five-minute `ready_readonly`. Labels are reported/configured client names, not verified models. Installed single-owner fixture and Gemini admission/removal are verified; fresh optional-permission prompt appearance and installed multi-owner behavior are unverified.

T3d adds **Unconfirmed Sends** with on-demand **Refresh status** and per-attempt **Sent** / **Not sent** choices. It lists at most eight eligible exact-chat operation IDs/timestamps, never message text. Choices record immutable person assessments only; they do not refill, resend, clear a draft or change grants. Isolated fixture/routed-Gemini checks pass, including stale/malformed replies and content-page refusal. The empty state means nothing is currently eligible to resolve, not proof that no uncertainty exists. Installed fixture empty-status acceptance passed; installed outcome choices remain untested. T3f connects Gemini page-sent resolution; T3k/T3l add bounded autonomous page assessment.

T3e retains exact-composer coordination after fill readback: another operation cannot consume fill consent while a live operation is filling, filled or fill-uncertain on the same origin/conversation/tab/document. It also covers competing operations of one owner and ordinary-review expiry while preparation remains live. Independent composers stay available after the shared pending-fill slot clears. Revocation, preparation expiry, definite failed fill or known dispatch outcome release coordination, not write authority; unresolved durable intents still use the shared conversation barrier. No new lock token, lease extension, draft clearing or automatic replay was added.

T3g adds an internal autonomous mode: preparation uses only the live owned `ready` grant, separates idempotency from supervised mode and keeps the original request expiry, at most 30 seconds. `acquireComposer` allows one queued wait per owner, FIFO per exact composer, independent composers, owned release and cancellation on removal/close/expiry. Reservations also block legacy fills before consuming consent. Neutral ledger fill/proof jobs require that reservation, retain four-second leases and offer once. They mint no supervised approval and appear as `prepared`, not ordinary review candidates. Gated T3h extends broker/native/worker phase transport and direct autonomous intent admission.

T3i adds `commitAutonomousMessage`: one owned composer reservation spans fill, challenged read, exact proof, durable intent and submit under the original deadline. Duplicate in-flight calls share the result; completed replay reads current durable status, while rejected attempts cannot restart. A second owner waits through the active submit and continues only after known evidence; an unresolved result stops it.

T3j adds strict facade-only `prepare_chat_message`, returning the status receipt before any browser job, and derives the existing neutral commit's mode from the owned ledger operation. The client accepts an explicit original send deadline up to 30 seconds for only those two calls; ordinary five-second requests and four-second browser leases are unchanged. Disposable fixture and routed-synthetic Gemini each pass one popup connection, one autonomous send, evidence-backed status, empty composer, persistent reservation and no re-offer.

T3k holds the composer after uncertain submit completion while reconciling retained provider evidence for at most five seconds or the original deadline. Delayed exact outgoing rows can resolve without another activation; no evidence, gaps, expiry or authority loss remain conservative. Browser coverage includes counted delayed append, lost script result and lost native submit completion for both providers.

T3l adds a distinct one-offer read-only `settle` job after the old activation lease ends, within that same settlement budget. Exact retained draft and available Send proof followed by a fresh challenged read and continuous complete unchanged rows yield immutable page-attributed `not_sent`. Gaps, edits, omissions, truncation, expired/missing proof, unavailable reads or revoked authority stay uncertain. It neither clears the draft nor restores consumed writes.

T3m adds public `chat_send_message { connectionId, text, idempotencyKey }` with `sent`, `not_sent` or `unresolved`, always `retryAllowed: false`. It reuses neutral preparation/commit inside one original 30-second deadline, returning the receipt when prepared. Exact owned durable-key replay reads status only, including after grant removal while original metadata remains; changed arguments conflict. Late expected errors prefer durable status, and transport loss after commit begins stays unresolved. Current installed evidence is summarized above; source coverage alone is not deployment.

T3n records successful neutral fills in one document-local isolated-world tracker, bound to operation, exact URL, root/editor objects and exact trusted insertion/readback. Input, retyping identical text, relevant mutations, observed mismatches, replacement, visibility loss and pagehide invalidate it; the known textarea insertion's deferred blur-change is handled separately. It stores no durable draft data, grants no write authority and clears nothing. Public synthetic browser cases verify retained provenance and human-retyping invalidation for both providers.

T3o adds bounded D10a replacement inside a fresh operation's existing one-shot fill, not a retry or separate clear command. The broker alone selects a newest eligible filled, resolved-not-sent prior autonomous operation owned by the same facade and connection on the exact composer/URL, while original metadata remains unexpired. The worker repeats read-only preflight and the page function rechecks ownership after focus/selection, then consumes the old marker for one selected native insertion. Human/unknown drafts and unresolved or expired prior metadata are never replacement authority. No new durable text, consent, job phase or lease extension is added.

T3p makes public send transport failures conservative even during preparation: a same-key duplicate may be waiting behind another invocation's durable commit. The SDK/broker regression covers both providers, one intent, no invented not-sent resolution, no re-offered jobs and fresh approval required after cancellation closes the owning connection. Explicit broker refusals and successful status replies retain their existing meanings.

T3q verifies the existing visible-tab boundary without Playwright's focus emulation. Disposable sandboxed Chromium and the public `noDefaults` CDP attachment prove a visible active chat can send while another window is focused. An inactive genuinely hidden chat preserves a human draft and creates no intent, browser submit reservation or activation. This is test-only infrastructure; no product permission, browser-control surface or hidden-tab sending is added.

T3r holds a successful browser fill or submit result, removes access through the trusted popup before that job's lease expires, then releases the result. After fill, the exact draft remains and no intent/reservation/activation exists. After activation, the one durable intent remains unresolved, without an invented resolution. Both providers lose the stored grant, refuse independently keyed new work and offer no further jobs. Recovery remains status only.

T3s repeats those browser boundaries with actual SDK cancellation and a queued same-key call. The duplicate stays unresolved even before intent; after activation there is one intent/activation and no invented resolution. Reconnected owners have no old approval or operation access. On trusted roster refresh, a validated exact-chat zero-owner reply clears only the unchanged autonomous local grant, guarded by document/URL/revision; other connected owners and legacy grants remain intact.

T4a adds `chat_wait_for_reply { connectionId, timeoutMs? }`, bounded to 20 seconds through existing owned fresh reads. Advancing captures must show an unchanged epoch/sequence and rendered window for two seconds; the newest row must be incoming and untruncated. Gaps, revisions, unavailable captures, outgoing tails and stale/backward timestamps reset evidence. Only `settled_heuristic` is reported, never confirmed completion; an existing newest reply may qualify. Ordinary reads now respect shorter upstream deadlines without expiring their transport/approval.

T4b uses reviewed positive Gemini busy/Stop evidence to mark only the newest incoming row `streaming`. The existing strict native/broker/read/event path preserves the marker, and indicator-only changes advance cursors. Quiet tracking resets while streaming and requires a new full quiet interval after it clears; no missing control or copy action yields `complete`.

T4c adds the reviewed UI-complete shape: newest incoming response, absent root busy attribute, exactly two visible response-owned descendants explicitly false, one enabled Copy action, no Stop/progress contradiction. Fresh untruncated UI-complete rows can satisfy the wait without heuristic delay. Other shapes retain unknown/streaming and the quiet fallback; no UI result establishes provider delivery.

Autonomous same-chat reload preserves existing handles after fresh new-document identity verification, invalidates observation cursors and reinstalls observers. Old-document reads/publications are blocked during suspension; removal/disconnect still work. Only the exact URL/chat/tab may rebind; legacy reload still revokes. Failure or uncertainty disconnects pinned targets, without new approval, automatic readmission or job replay. Bounded installed fixture and Gemini reloads retained the same handle and produced fresh cursor epochs; failure/race coverage remains synthetic, not broad installed reliability evidence.

### Critical path to the target

Done: G1, the dispatch-job slice (`11dec64`), and T1 (R1/R2 read robustness). Steps T1-T6 replace the earlier G2-G6 plan. The exact changes, tests, completion criteria and ask-points for each step are in **Implementation Plan T1-T6** below the dashboard.

1. **T1 Read robustness: done.** Stopped native watches restart with bounded backoff (R2). D8 captures the newest 32 visible rows with `omittedBefore`, per-message truncation at 16,000 UTF-16 units and a 96 KiB capture cap. Identity-preserving failures skip publication; changed targets still revoke (R1). Synthetic evidence only for these changes.
2. **T2 Same-chat reload passed installed; multi-owner acceptance remains.** Source repairs retain the current full gate and verified ZIP. Latest separately approved read-only Gemini reload keeps the original connection, resets the cursor epoch and returns the matching complete/untruncated newest reply; disconnect/stale ends scope. Earlier failed reload/gates remain history, not explained common causes. Installed multi-owner admission/removal needs independent MCP owners, separate popup approvals and a fresh bounded scope; no automatic admission or relaxed limits.
3. **T3 Autonomous send: synthetic target and bounded installed D9a rollover acceptance passed.** Latest 18-turn run passed the first and two subsequent 32-row rollovers on one connection. Forty-two synthetic combinations, old refusal coverage and partial-window not-sent refusal remain gated. Original deadlines, ownership, one-offer jobs, reservation and activation rules unchanged. This did not retry or resolve the separate earlier uncertain send; broad reliability is not established.
4. **T4 Reply completion: source/synthetic target gated (T4a-T4c); latest 18 installed waits passed.** Each returned a new UI-complete row with a matching later fresh read. Older timeout/unavailable-read and weather unknown-completion evidence remain with unproven causes. R21's post-encoding read reserve/false-owner-teardown repair now passes deterministic red/green and the complete 248-unit/134-named-E2E gate. No deadline or completion rule weakened; UI completion does not prove provider delivery or faithful mathematical-text extraction.
5. **T5 Planned visible-chat acceptance: passed on one selected Gemini chat.** Fresh bounded permission, empty-composer confirmation and one popup approval supported three PAGE_SENT turns and three UI-complete replies without further approvals. The person confirmed the three cycles and empty composer, then closed the tab; the old connection became stale and pickup empty. This does not establish broad reliability or hidden-tab operation. The session is ended; earlier failures and their unproven causes remain history. D6's subsequent probe/permission removal is source/synthetic gated, not installed-accepted.
6. **T6 Image-backed root repair committed/full-gated; covered installed calibration passed.** Source `9d34083` retains its exact gate/verified artifacts. Existing-sample two-read pass plus separately approved new four-case send/wait/read calibration preserve simple/indexed/nested roots and mixed script/fraction structure, complete/untruncated with stable later captures. Latest active work 47.1 seconds; total including coordination 190.3 seconds, within original new limits; disconnect/stale ends all scope. No complete/numerical fidelity, delivery claim or unchanged-gate rerun. Earlier failures/uncertain send stay intact.
7. **Databag (D14-D20): native source/synthetic checkpoint passed; installed ABI remains.** Six public native cases, real-React/continuity checks and R22 IPC/browser checks pass the exact 248-unit/141-named-E2E gate. Frozen inputs and private ZIP/output bytes agree. Earlier Gemini timeout, startup/relay and overlapping missing-manifest failures stay recorded, not explained by the later pass. Deployed ABI/loaded Build and D16 remain unverified; no automatic live action.

### Findings: functional

- **R1 Visible offscreen capture defect reproduced/repaired and gated.** Empty auto-contained offscreen query text could invalidate the full snapshot; bounded fallback and synthetic scrolling/privacy coverage pass. Latest installed reads passed eighteen turns and rolling 32-row captures. Offscreen state/fallback use were not measured; this remains a plausible mechanism, not a demonstrated diagnosis of the older third-turn incident. The separate send boundary is R10 below.
- **R2 Fixed (T1a).** Both native watches schedule at most one restart timer after timeout, parse failure or port disconnect: 1 s doubling to 10 s, reset after a valid reply. The timer rechecks tracked unexpired grants before connecting. Disposable-browser checks verify fixture and Gemini reads recover without reapproval and a consumed Gemini fill job is not re-offered.
- **R3 Bounded installed Gemini same-chat reload passed.** On the person-confirmed current Build, original request/connection stays ready after the person's reload without reconnecting. Fresh newest-reply capture matches baseline, complete/untruncated, on a new cursor epoch; disconnect/stale ends scope. Local delayed-render red/green and lifecycle/full-gate coverage remain valid. Earlier installed failure's exact cause and broad reliability remain unproven; no automatic reconnection or replacement-chat approval.
- **R4 Fixed synthetically (T3m); planned Gemini live flow passed.** One popup connection approval supported three autonomous sends/replies without per-message review/fill/Send consents; the person confirmed the visible result. Tab closure ended that connection. The supervised fixture path retains separate controls. This is bounded single-chat acceptance, not broad reliability evidence.

### Findings: architecture and maintainability

- **A1 Provider duplication.** Parallel legacy ledger/client/native/worker command families remain, while the popup now selects generic controls/handlers through one capability table. Public autonomous sending, roster/removal/disconnect, preparation, commits, bounded page-sent/not-sent settlement and all autonomous job phases are provider-neutral. Several fixture-named items remain provider-generic (`disconnect_fixture`, `revoke_all_fixture`, `revokeTrackedFixture`, `completeFixtureBrowserCheck`, shared pending caps). Do not add a provider by copying (D5).
- **A2 D6 resolved and gated.** The person chose removal of both legacy input probes and required debugger permission on 2026-10-19. Probe UI/routes/payload and permission are removed; native-bridge diagnostics and the connected `execCommand("insertText")` / guarded `click()` writer remain. Exact source/synthetic gate passes; installed D6 acceptance has not run. The older writer passed planned live visible-chat acceptance. Hidden-tab input and any new driver/permission remain separately scoped.
- **A3 Model gaps.** `generation` is always 1; reload replaces observation epochs rather than handles. Popup-first grants report `ready`, read/send capabilities and no expiry; legacy grants remain `ready_readonly` with per-operation write consent. Autonomous sends and the conversation uncertainty barrier are connected, but connection metadata has no explicit paused state. The popup has IDs, labels, per-agent admission/removal and the data-boundary disclosure. Owners are facade sockets, not automatically distinct models or VS Code chat sessions (DESIGN.md section 7). The extension mirrors broker schemas by hand, so protocol changes must be made in both places.
- **A4 Port churn reduced.** Snapshot publication, job completion and shared review/roster/reload requests now reuse a bounded worker control port with one-second idle cleanup and no failed-request replay. Read watches remain independent; the earlier speculative watch-port reuse experiment remains removed. Some legacy one-shot commands still open their own ports. See DEVELOPMENT.md, Native Request Channel.
- **A5 Native Databag ABI/installed compatibility remains unverified.** D20's gated native bridge removes the marker dependency for reviewed synthetics; the historical marker adapter remains only for its fixtures. Readiness depends on reviewed React/SDK shapes and fails closed on unknown ones, not an attested deployed ABI. Locator calibration cannot establish identity or turn connection-local tokens into provider-stable IDs. Do not patch the website; see DESIGN.md section 8 and D16.

### Findings: process and documentation

- Earlier review snapshot, not a current repository count: 139 commits in 15 days. Each boundary (ledger, private transport, trusted UI, worker, public tool) was a separate gated checkpoint with a long handoff entry (D7).
- This file is about 260 KB. Before the review, two documentation lines exceeded the 2,000-character limit of agent file readers, and DEVELOPMENT.md was mostly dated history. The review added this dashboard, split those lines, rewrote DEVELOPMENT.md as a reference and added readability rules to AGENTS.md.
- P1 keeps focused per-edit feedback and the unchanged full checkpoint gate. The companion now builds once per gate; standalone build-and-unit use is `test:unit:build`. A parser-checked E2E split preserves every original test/helper body. Bounded scheduling and failed benchmark evidence are below; power state affects timing, so do not attribute the whole observed improvement to code.

### Decisions

Decided on 2026-10-09: D1-D5 and D7-D13 (DESIGN.md section 17). D13a/b revisions allow several separately approved agents per chat; D10a permits only proven own-leftover replacement after uncertainty is resolved. D6 decided 2026-10-19: remove legacy probes/debugger permission. D11a separately approved 2026-10-19: synthetic-first exact autonomous hidden operations with the existing writer, no activation/new permission and unchanged other authority/evidence/provenance boundaries. D14 selects standard self-hosted Databag as the second site (October 28); this is not live-test permission.

D15's patch direction is superseded by the person's no-website-changes constraint (October 28). D16 is open: establish safe native binding, then explicitly decide any narrower Databag connection lifetime if cross-reload identity is unavailable. No exception to existing authority or lifecycle rules is implied.

D9a decided 2026-10-20: the person selected bounded rolling-window evidence after disclosure of changes outside the captured window being unobservable. That choice authorized synthetic implementation only; the latest installed retest used separate bounded approval, now ended. The precise contract is in DESIGN.md section 10. D14 supersedes the earlier Gemini-only focus for the new Databag adapter work; existing Gemini behavior remains supported.

The requested source commit/push is complete (`e9790d3`); its exact gate and private package are verified. Do not rerun the unchanged successful gate. Keep the person's Chromium closed. The next technical boundary is installed native compatibility, requiring fresh bounded authorization, person-performed extension reload/full Build confirmation and exact-chat popup approval. ## Implementation Plan T1-T6 (2026-10-09)

Work top to bottom. Each source checkpoint follows AGENTS.md: focused checks first, then the exact gate, a short dated entry, the dashboard updated in place, and DEVELOPMENT.md updated whenever tools, limits or setup change. Keep the fixture and Gemini behaving alike unless a step says otherwise. Stop and ask the user only at the marked points or when a human step is needed.

### T1 Read robustness (no user decision needed)

**T1a Restart a stopped native watch (R2).**

- Code: `startGeminiReadWatch` and `startFixtureReadWatch` in `packages/extension/entrypoints/background.ts`. Their `stop()` ends the watch for good today.
- Change: when a watch stops while `hasTrackedGeminiGrant()` (or `hasTrackedFixtureGrant()`) still returns true, schedule one restart with bounded backoff, for example 1 s doubling to 10 s and reset after a successful reply. Keep at most one pending restart timer, and do not restart when no tracked grant remains.
- Why it is safe: a restart only resumes polling. The broker never re-offers an issued fill, proof or submit job, and lost results already settle uncertain.
- Test: in a disposable browser, end the watch's native port (for example kill its long-lived `native-relay` process, or force a reply timeout), then show that a later `chat_read_messages` succeeds without reapproval and that no issued job is offered twice.

**T1b Observation window and failure policy (D8, R1).**

- Capture: in `captureGeminiSnapshot` (`packages/extension/lib/gemini-observation.ts`) and `captureFixtureSnapshot` (`packages/extension/lib/fixture-observation.ts`), keep the newest 32 visible rows instead of failing. Cut any message longer than 16,000 UTF-16 units and mark it `truncated: true`. Then drop the oldest rows until the JSON is at most 96 KiB, cutting the newest row further only if it alone is too big. Report `omittedBefore: true` whenever rows were dropped. Rows with ambiguous structure still make the capture fail.
- Failure policy: the Gemini `gemini_messages_changed` handler and the watch's challenge loop in `background.ts` revoke the grant on any failed publication. Revoke only when `classifyGeminiPublicationFailure` returns `target_changed`; on `observation_unavailable`, skip the publication and keep the grant, so a challenged read times out as `OBSERVATION_UNAVAILABLE`. Give the fixture handlers the same identity classification.
- Broker and schemas: change the caps and add the optional per-message `truncated: true` and the capture-level `omittedBefore` in `pending-connections.ts` (`MAX_*_SNAPSHOT_BYTES`, the 2,048 text checks in both publishers, per-grant buffers from 32 events/256 KiB to 128 events/4 MiB), `broker-requests.ts` (publish and message schemas), `native-protocol.ts` (publish parsers) and `broker-client.ts` (reply schemas, including event payloads). In `broker-ipc.ts`, `omittedBefore` must also be true when the capture omitted rows. Two 96 KiB events must still fit one 256 KiB frame.
- Tests: unit checks for the new bounds, strict refusal of other extra fields and deduplication that includes the new flags. Synthetic-page checks for more than 32 rows, a 16,001-unit message, a transient model row without `model-response-content` (no revocation, and a later publication works) and a URL change (still revokes).
- Done when: the exact gate passes, the dashboard marks R1 and R2 fixed, and the DEVELOPMENT.md limits and troubleshooting entries are updated.

### T2 Connection model (multi-agent D13a and per-agent approval D13b decided)

- **Broker start (D4):** `packages/companion/src/mcp-stdio.ts` connects at startup. If the socket is missing or refuses, it spawns `broker-process.js` detached with `stdio: "ignore"` (never the MCP stdout), waits within a bounded loop for the owner-only private socket and connects. Racing facades are safe because the broker refuses a second instance. `mcp-stdio.test.ts` currently expects `BROKER_UNAVAILABLE` without a broker (around line 161); keep a way to disable auto-start in tests.
- **Connect this chat (D13, D1):** add the popup action for an eligible fixture or Gemini tab. Its text states that the agent may read and send in this chat on its own until the tab closes, and that chat content can influence what it sends. The click requests optional host permission for that origin (`optional_host_permissions` in `packages/extension/wxt.config.ts`; `permissions.request` must run inside the click handler), then a new relay-only command creates an autonomous grant.
- **D13a, D13b (2026-10-12):** allow several agents on one chat with distinct owned grants, each approved once in the trusted popup. No exclusive pickup, several-facade refusal, automatic admission of later arrivals or silent re-admission after revocation. The person approves an identifiable current owner before pickup; a restarted or removed owner needs fresh approval. Same-chat reload preserves approved connections (D2).
- **Roster and revocation:** the trusted popup shows the connected count, distinguishable display labels and broker-minted instance IDs, status, **Remove access** per agent and **Disconnect all**. An agent cannot revoke another agent. Individual removal denies that owner's future reads/writes, cancels unstarted work and prevents silent re-admission; other owners stay connected. Retain uncertain in-flight status, never promise to undo an already activated send. Current owners are facade sockets, not automatically separate models or VS Code chat sessions (DESIGN.md section 7).
- **Pickup tool:** `chat_wait_for_connection { timeoutMs }` (at most 20 s) returns connections offered to this agent. `chat_request_connection` stays for tests.
- **Grant model:** autonomous grants report state `ready` with read and send capability and no expiry (`expiresAt: null`). Keep clipping correct wherever expiry feeds deadlines: `liveGrant`, read challenges, `getConnectionExpiresAt` and the extension's stored grants. Legacy read-only grants keep the five-minute TTL.
- **Lifetime (D2):** tab close, chat switch, extension reload, browser restart (`ensureFixtureReset`) and owner disconnect already revoke. Change the `tabs.onUpdated` handler near the end of `background.ts` so a reload of the same exact URL re-attaches: after load, confirm identity in the new document, rebind the broker target through a new relay-only command, mark an observation gap and reinstall observers. Any other URL change still revokes.
- **Tests:** units and SDK checks for several facades receiving distinct handles on one chat, independent reads/cursors, strict owner-bound request/status/pickup, roster count/labels, duplicate display names distinguished by instance IDs, one owner's disconnect/restart and individual human removal leaving others intact, denied cross-owner revocation and no silent re-admission. Retain no-expiry/deadline/rebind/gap cases. Disposable-browser tests cover Connect/admission per D13b, roster/removal, same-chat reload, chat switch and tab close ending all target grants.
- **Done when:** the gate passes, DEVELOPMENT.md documents the new flow and tools, and the capability matrix is updated.

### T3 Autonomous send (D10a decided 2026-10-13)

- **Leftover draft (D10a):** the system may clear only its own exactly matching unsent leftover draft before a new operation, with fresh exact-target/ownership evidence. Text equality alone is insufficient. Never clear a person's draft, clear while a prior submit is unresolved, retry that operation or restore consumed authority. This policy decision is not a live-test grant.
- **Tool:** `chat_send_message { connectionId, text, idempotencyKey }` on an autonomous connection. Results: `sent` (D9 evidence); `not_sent` with a code, after which the agent may send again with a new key; or `unresolved`, which pauses sending in that chat. Every result says `retryAllowed: false` for that operation, and the same key and text return the existing status.
- **Pipeline:** in the broker, inside one absolute deadline: prepare (journal digest and receipt), fill job, fresh challenged read, proof job, durable intent, submit job with browser reservation and one guarded activation, then evidence. Add a dedicated budget for this request only (for example 30 s); every inner lease stays at most 4 s and is clipped to it. Owner disconnect before durable intent sends nothing; after it, the outcome is still recorded.
- **Provider-neutral (D5):** implement the pipeline once, with provider adapters for text limits, editor kind and evidence. Reuse `fillGeminiDraft`, `inspectGeminiDraft` and `submitGeminiDraft`, the worker executors and `reserveBrowserSubmitAttempt` (Gemini namespace). Use generic phase commands for new browser jobs instead of more `*_gemini_*` families. Relax the staged job's post-offer cursor check (see the Staged Gemini One-Offer Dispatch Job entry).
- **Evidence and unconfirmed sends (D9, D10):** after activation, watch for a settle window of about 5 s. Baseline rows followed by exactly one new outgoing row with the exact text means sent. The exact text still in the composer with Send enabled and no new row, checked with `inspectGeminiDraft` in prepared mode, means not sent. Anything else is unresolved.
- **Barrier (R8):** scope `recordDispatchIntent` to one conversation. Store on each attempt a conversation key: an HMAC of origin and conversation ID under a persistent 32-byte key kept in a new single-row table of the same private database, because `digestKey` is random per process. Record resolutions (`sent` or `not_sent`, by page or person) in a new table. An attempt without evidence or resolution blocks only its own conversation; older attempts without a key keep the current global behavior.
- **Popup:** for a connected chat, list unresolved sends with **Sent** and **Not sent** buttons through new relay-only commands, plus **Disconnect**.
- **Runaway guard:** one send in flight (the shared pending-submit cap already allows only one globally) and an hourly cap per conversation, counted from journal attempts and set by an environment variable such as `BROWSER_CHAT_MAX_SENDS_PER_HOUR` (default 60).
- **Shared chat (revised D13a):** serialize the entire fill/proof/submit/evidence sequence across all owners of the same composer, not just script calls. Concurrent send requests stay bounded and owner-specific; no interleaved drafts, duplicate activation or automatic uncertain retry. The hourly cap and unresolved-send pause are shared by the conversation. Test two owners contending for the same chat, revocation while waiting/in flight, and uncertainty pausing every writer on that chat without ending other chats.
- **Visible tab (D11):** the Gemini write executors (`fillTrackedGeminiDraft`, `checkTrackedGeminiDraft` and the new submit executor) require the active tab of the current window via `tabs.query({ active: true, currentWindow: true })`. Require instead that the target tab is active in its own window (`tabs.get(tabId).active`) and visible. Popup approvals keep the current-window check.
- **Tests:** units for pipeline states, idempotency, guard, per-conversation barrier and resolution. Disposable-browser tests on synthetic Gemini pages for sent, not sent, unresolved with popup resolution, hidden-tab refusal, and the existing loss cases (script result loss, native completion loss, worker stop and wake, broker kill), always with at most one activation.
- **Done when:** the gate passes, and DEVELOPMENT.md, the dashboard and README.md (outgoing messages no longer need separate approval) are updated.

### T4 Reply completion (needs a live read-only review)

- Build `settled_heuristic` first: the newest incoming row unchanged for a quiet period of about 2 s under continuous observation.
- Ask the user to authorize a fresh read-only review of one disposable Gemini chat while it streams, to find a reviewed completion indicator such as the Send/Stop control state. Record only fixed metadata. Then report `streaming`, `complete` or `settled_heuristic` on the newest incoming row.
- Add `chat_wait_for_reply { connectionId, timeoutMs }` (at most 20 s; the agent calls again), returning the newest incoming reply once it is complete or settled.
- Done when: the gate passes and the tools are documented.

### T5 Live acceptance (human steps and authorization)

- Ask the user to authorize one bounded session (AGENTS.md): a disposable chat, the planned neutral messages and a message limit.
- Coordinated restart (DEVELOPMENT.md) and extension reload; the person opens the chat in container Chromium and clicks **Connect this chat**.
- The agent picks up the connection, sends the planned messages and reads the complete replies with no further clicks. The person then closes the tab, and the connection must end.
- Record outcomes and timings only, never chat text, URLs or IDs. Then decide D6 with the user.

### T6 Later

Hidden-tab operation (D11; separately review any new driver/permission under D6), a second chat site on the provider-neutral pipeline, calibration and packaging. D6's current-mode probe/permission removal is a separate checkpoint below; it does not enable hidden input.

## T3b Durable Shared-Conversation Hourly Guard (2026-10-13)

- `BROWSER_CHAT_MAX_SENDS_PER_HOUR` configures the broker's positive safe-integer limit (default 60); no MCP argument can change it. ## Staged Gemini One-Offer Dispatch Job (2026-10-09)

- This slice was written in the previous session and left uncommitted; it was reviewed and committed unchanged at the user's request. Internal `requestGeminiDispatch` requires an owned approved Gemini operation, distinct Send authorization and a completed fresh proof. It records the durable intent through `recordGeminiDispatchStart` first, then creates one volatile job whose lease is the minimum of the original deadline, the Send consent and the proof lease.
- `listGeminiDispatchAttempts` issues the job once and never re-offers it. `completeGeminiDispatch` accepts only the strict `geminiDispatchResultSchema` result for the exact issued target and attempt, and always settles `dispatch_uncertain` because no Gemini evidence rule exists yet (D9). Fixture and Gemini jobs share the existing one-pending-submit cap in both directions. Release, expiry and disconnect settle uncertain. There is no transport, worker or public route.
- Three existing-file units cover the positive one-offer path (invalid deadlines, foreign owner, wrong target or attempt, an extra delivery claim, replay, receipt privacy), nine loss, expiry, owner, revocation, restart, cursor and refused-result modes, and the shared slot without consuming the waiting provider's consent.
- Review note: after the offer, freshness still requires an unchanged cursor and a baseline under four seconds old. A real Send changes the page, so its completion would be refused and settle uncertain; the fixture checks freshness only before offering. T3 must relax this together with D9. The outcome is uncertain either way, so the current behavior is conservative, not unsafe.
- The focused units passed **3/3** during the review. The exact gate `npm run build && npm run typecheck && npm run test:unit && npm run test:e2e` then passed once: build, both typechecks, **170/170 units** (about 20 seconds) and **20/20 e2e checks** (about 82 seconds). ## Lazy Wire Decoding Checkpoint (2026-10-06)

- A parser-count regression reproduced all 64 batch frames being decoded before a consumer could refuse its first frame. The decoder now offers a synchronous lazy `frames()` iterator; its existing array-returning `push()` delegates to that iterator and remains compatible. Broker and native input handlers use the iterator, so their existing overload or validation refusal stops further parsing instead of retaining a whole decoded batch.
- Frame state is reset before yielding each complete message. Consumers must exhaust the iterator or close the refused stream; both wire handlers already close/invalidate on refusal. This is sequential frame handling, not atomic processing of an OS chunk: an authenticated hello can be answered before a malformed later frame is read. ## Deadline-Preservation Checkpoint (2026-10-01)

This internal follow-up is separate from clock repair. A native request was validated before waiting in the relay queue, but forwarding later minted fresh broker budgets. The pending change preserves the original absolute deadline through that boundary; it never extends a budget or authorizes an expired request.

- `brokerRequestDeadline` clips the existing five-second local budget to an optional validated upstream cap, rejects expired/malformed/excessive caps before filesystem access, and is checked again before authentication and each queued request. Broker timers use the clipped remaining duration. Native relay forwarding supplies its original request deadline; default facade clients retain their existing five-second budgets.
- Authenticated hello metadata now carries only role, request ID, and the already-validated deadline, never the credential. The broker echoes that deadline instead of producing an independent ten-second response budget. Clients require exact reply correlation and reject replies received after expiry. The first integration check exposed this old handshake mismatch; it was repaired at the owning broker/helper boundary, not by removing correlation checks.
- Both fixture socket tests now acquire connections inside their existing cleanup blocks and track successfully opened clients. A later setup/authentication failure therefore closes earlier clients, broker, database, and temporary directory instead of leaving listeners running. The failed pre-repair test run was stopped deliberately; no test failure was hidden or turned into a pass.
- Native broker failures log only allowlisted fixed reasons on stderr; unknown/schema errors use fixed categories. No arbitrary exception message, credential, recovery token, text, or browser identity is logged. Stdout remains framed protocol output, and public errors remain `BROKER_UNAVAILABLE`. Strict synthetic framing assertions include these diagnostics without weakening expected kinds/refusals.
- Deterministic deadline arithmetic passed **1/1**; the repaired role/socket/native suites passed **25/25**. A first exact full-gate attempt passed build/typecheck and **92/93 units**, then stopped at a native send-consent reply returning `error`; e2e was not reached. The isolated framing case passed, but a later focused 25-case run passed **24/25** with `BROKER_UNAVAILABLE` instead of `SEND_REVIEW_UNAVAILABLE`. Its exact cause is not established. After cleanup/diagnostic follow-ups the isolated framing case passed again. Two final exact gate attempts passed build, both typechecks, and **93/93 units**, then **11/12 browser tests**: popup review-list and approval-confirmation waits timed out respectively. Between them the unchanged focused popup flow passed **1/1**. This is not a full-green result; no source checkpoint commit is made.
- The seven deadline-related source/test files remain unstaged on top of `9f5786a`; only this findings/status record is committed. No host-clock diagnostic or source switch was run, no timeout/assertion was relaxed, and no installed profile or real site was accessed. Continue other independently testable tasks if useful; require a green exact gate before committing this pending slice. Do not infer that either the clock or browser timing failures are repaired.

## Internal Guard Checkpoint (2026-10-01)

This checkpoint completes the internal consent and journal hardening on top of `2abf3e3`. The user explicitly selected **Continue internal hardening; defer commits**, changing the original work order without relaxing any deadline, full-gate requirement, or synthetic-only restriction. The pending consent policy and documentation were preserved, not reset. The final exact gate is green, meeting the requirement for the authorized scoped commit.

- `recordFixtureDispatchStart` now rejects ordinary no-send approval and past filled metadata without distinct send consent. It also requires a persisted recovery receipt, a mandatory baseline captured at or after that send consent and less than four seconds old, an unchanged observation epoch/sequence, and a completed exact-document draft check. `BEGIN IMMEDIATE`, the unresolved-writer reservation, and the only durable intent per operation remain intact.
- Internal-only `requestFixtureDispatchCheck` / `completeFixtureDispatchCheck` bind a fresh random check ID to the owner, immutable text/target, baseline, and a deadline clipped to four seconds, consent expiry, and baseline expiry. Strict completion requires exact current draft text, a supported editor, selected/writable controls, and ready submit controls. Foreign/changed targets, altered drafts, invalid result fields, old/replaced checks, baseline replacement/gaps, future timestamps, and expiry fail closed. These methods have no broker/native/MCP command or browser worker handler; they do not themselves inspect or submit in a browser. Their production completion must eventually come only from a trusted fresh browser check, never an agent or page claim.
- Only after SQLite commit returns does the ledger create a private memory-only dispatch authorization. `consumeFixtureDispatchAuthorization` permits one owner-bound consumption, using the durable operation ID as its future browser deduplication key. It rechecks live target, intent, deadline, baseline/cursor, and absence of UI evidence. Expiry, disconnect, revocation, restart, clock rewind, changed observations, UI echoes, and prior consumption lose authority without retry; receipts recover only status. Failed journal insertion creates no authorization. No browser submit action or delivery claim exists.
- Existing crash/replay/writer/evidence tests now satisfy the stronger guards. Broker/socket/official-SDK recovery tests seed synthetic durable artifacts instead of creating live authority through the obsolete no-send start API. The duplicate-row Chromium test uses separate synthetic consents, actual fixture draft readback, and the prepared-draft inspector before guarded intent and one authorization consumption; its own test UI still performs both submissions. It is not connector submission or real-toolbar send approval.
- `inspectFixturePreflight` now has a pure read-only `draftState: "prepared"` mode requiring the current visible editor text to exactly match immutable prepared text. Omitted/`"empty"` mode retains the existing empty-editor requirement; malformed modes, altered/empty prepared drafts, inactive documents, blocked controls, and changed targets fail closed. It never focuses, edits, clicks, or returns draft text. Both textarea and rich-editor tests preserve text, focus, selection, timeline, and scroll. This primitive has no dispatch-check worker/native handler, and existing private preflight traffic still uses empty mode.
- Lifecycle test setup now waits for the existing popup startup/reset path before asserting reload revocation, and for initial navigation to finish on every synthetic tab before seeding its fake grant. This generalizes the existing disconnect-tab wait. Exact denial codes, revocation expectations, and deadlines remain unchanged; no production lifecycle route was altered.

In that run WXT's timestamps went from 10:26:24 to 10:28:19 and back to 10:26:25, with a reported **-114,314 ms** build duration. This is clock-instability evidence outside the ledger; the exact cause remains unresolved. A separate 24-child wall-clock probe did not reproduce disagreement.

Both new failures passed **2/2** in isolation. A three-million-reading Node wall-clock/monotonic probe found no jumps; there was no configured preload clock hook.
- **External clock fault reproduced:** a standalone C probe outside the repository compared adjacent libc `gettimeofday` and direct `SYS_gettimeofday` readings for approximately ten seconds. Across **66,528,899** samples it found **four disagreements**, in both directions, up to **114,923,828 microseconds** (about 114.9 seconds), without Node or any project code. The WSL kernel is `6.18.33.2-microsoft-standard-WSL2`; its current clock source is `tsc`, with `hyperv_clocksource_tsc_page`, `hyperv_clocksource_msr`, and `acpi_pm` also available. This proves an external clock discrepancy, not its precise kernel/host cause. No clock-source or host setting was changed.
- **Blocker requiring host intervention:** repair/restart the WSL host clock, then rerun the exact full gate before making the requested scoped consent commit. A Windows-side `wsl --shutdown` followed by reopening the devcontainer is a first recovery attempt, not a confirmed fix; it stops all WSL workloads, including these fixture/broker services. Recheck services after reopening and do not start duplicates. Do not change host-wide clock configuration from the container or claim a green checkpoint from isolated passes. After the green consent commit, tighten the still-unexposed journal start to require distinct send consent, a mandatory fresh baseline and exact current-draft/document checks, durable intent before one submit, and deduplication. No-send review, fill consent, past fill metadata, recovery receipts, and UI echoes are not send/retry authority.
- On the latest continuation, all fixture/broker/Chromium/native/MCP services were stopped and the same five pending files remained unstaged at `2abf3e3`. The clock discrepancy persisted: a repeat probe found four disagreements up to **114,525,062 microseconds**. An extended probe additionally compared direct kernel realtime and monotonic syscalls; it found **four kernel offset jumps** in **40,603,818** samples, so a libc-only test wrapper cannot establish reliable time. No wrapper, deadline workaround, or application change was made.
- The user explicitly chose **Host clock diagnostic**, authorizing a temporary host-wide switch from `tsc` to the advertised `hyperv_clocksource_tsc_page` source. The human must run `printf '%s\n' hyperv_clocksource_tsc_page | sudo tee /sys/devices/system/clocksource/clocksource0/current_clocksource` in a **WSL host terminal, not the devcontainer**. Approval is recorded, but execution and recovery are not yet confirmed. After the human reports completion, recheck the source, rerun the bounded probe and exact full gate, and proceed only if green. Synthetic socket/native/browser tests cover empty readiness, preserved user drafts, disabled submit controls, inactive selection, and unchanged approved state. The test-copy extension has temporary fixture host permission and is not real-toolbar coverage.
- The browser inspector supports only the literal default fixture and `?editor=rich` URLs, exact `fixture-alpha` identity, one writable empty editor, and one visible unobstructed submit button. Drafts (including whitespace), read-only/inert/inherited-disabled controls, duplicate editors, overlays, and changed targets return fixed codes without revealing draft text. Chromium's lone `<br>` cleared-rich-editor placeholder is accepted as empty; other existing nodes are not. Text is capped at 2,048 UTF-16 units/4,000 UTF-8 bytes and rejected if empty, padded, or containing carriage returns. The inspector itself never focuses, edits, scrolls, clicks, grants approval, or starts dispatch. Private watch replies now have four fields (`challenges`, `activeTabIds`, `preflightChecks`, `draftFills`), so broker/native/extension builds must be updated together; old readers fail closed.
- Recovery is **not** resend authorization: a receipt does not restore the previous session owner, browser grant, approval, text, or baseline. Unresolved intents still block another writer; legacy intents without receipts and interrupted attempts without a surviving baseline cannot be automatically reconciled. Do not infer "safe to retry" from `unknown` or clear an intent automatically. Next require a fresh baseline and browser preflight/fill before any fixture submit path, add appropriately scoped human send approval, then test crashes/lost responses around the actual synthetic fixture submit. The existing popup says **Approve draft (no send)**; do not silently reuse that approval for submission. A real-site send requires separate user approval.
- The native-host fixture subprocess has an unresolved intermittent deadline rejection (details below). Do not weaken its production deadline checks to force a passing test. The development broker or VS Code MCP process may predate local builds; restart them deliberately only for a needed test, never assume a running process has the latest schemas.

### What the user actually wants

An agent using MCP can read and send messages in **a browser chat conversation explicitly selected by the user**. The chat might be Gemini, WhatsApp Web, or another website. The user should be able to open the chat normally, keep their existing login, and connect it using an extension action.

Gemini is a convenient first AI-chat test target, not a requirement to integrate with Google's API. LM Studio was only an earlier example. Do not build an LM Studio integration, terminal chat client, native desktop automation tool, or unrestricted browser MCP server.

The eventual generic behavior comes from a shared chat model plus reviewed site adapters and a user-calibrated DOM profile. It does not mean every website can be supported without configuration or maintenance.
